# Language breadth and depth — the plan (vision 37, ADR-048)

Grammar v2 (`docs/plans/README.md`). A row flips to `[x]` only when
`fathom verify --service fathom --plan docs/plans/language-breadth.md` proves it.
Design: `docs/design/language-breadth.md` (section numbers below are its; §13 is the worker
recipe). Queue: `docs/plans/language-breadth.queue.json` — one `LB-Q-<id>` row per entry,
in the same order (`TestLanguageQueueMatchesPlan`, LB-F22, holds the two together).
Invariants I54 and I55 rise from `stated` as LB-F12, LB-F14, LB-F16 and LB-F17 land.

Rows name the contract they will be proven by: the function that will carry the behaviour
and the test that will reach it, or — for a language or tool format — a lexical oracle on
the committed reading whose `level_met` the harness computes (design §10). Nobody types a
level into a reading. Until the code and readings exist every row is open, and
`fathom verify` reports it open, never proven.

Row ids are stable. Foundation rows are `LB-F<n>` (Opus), measurements `LB-M<n>` (op), and
queue rows `LB-Q-<queue id>` (Sonnet workers, one per row, recipe §13). A queue row that
cannot reach its target keeps its contract and gains a `[-]` sibling
`LB-Q-<id>-rest` with a disposition (§14.2); the pattern is never loosened.

## F. Foundation — measurement first, then the engines (Opus)

### F.9 Route references (EXCELLENCE T5, wave px)

The runtime topology's first edges: a route, upstream or mesh rule to the Kubernetes Service or
compose service its target host names (design §4, "Route references"). Interim divergence from
ADR-050 (Revisit when: SG-2 lands): stored as file-tier typed edges under `route-ref`, each
carrying the `deploy:` key SG-21/SG-10 will write into `system_links`.

### F.12 Policy references (EXCELLENCE T4, wave pc)

A policy to what it governs (design §4, "Policy references"). F61 names `policy_ref` and forbids a
new edge table before RT-M1 and SG-2, so the edges are stored as route-ref's are: file-tier typed
edges under `policy-ref` (Revisit when: RT-M1 reads PASS and SG-2 lands). The kyverno and
gatekeeper items emit `resource-kind` and `label-selector` targets; the native and cloud items emit
the same contract (`resource-type`, `aws-arn`, `service-name` too) into the one resolver.

- [x] Kyverno and Gatekeeper files are claimed by API group before the Kubernetes verdict: a `.yaml`/`.yml` whose every object document is `kyverno.io`, `policies.kyverno.io` or a `cli.kyverno.io` Test reads through the kyverno dialect, `templates.gatekeeper.sh`, `constraints.gatekeeper.sh` or a `test.gatekeeper.sh` Suite through the gatekeeper dialect; a file mixing them with other objects stays Kubernetes; one hook, `policyDispatch`, in `resolveExtractor` and in `ReingestFile` (after gatewayDispatch), the dispatched extractor adding each match block's policy_ref targets {id: LB-F134, symbol: policyDispatch, package: internal/fusion, reachable_from: TestIngest_PolicyRefs, needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9"}

### F.16 IaC: Kubernetes ecosystem and Ansible (EXCELLENCE T3, wave iac)

Argo CD and Workflows, Flux, Crossplane and Ansible, four doc-engine dialects with cross-file edges at L3
(tier C, good: no offline tool prints an entity and a line). The three CR dialects are claimed by their
content sniff when EVERY document of a file is theirs (`crDispatch`); a mixed file stays Kubernetes whole
and is counted. Ansible is the classifier's last signal. Observations O405 to O409.

### F.18 Go call grading (wave le, item go)

Go's L2 was graded on goextract's syntactic Calls, a set no tool reads. It now grades the calls the product serves
(`callgraph.BuildBoth`: the syntactic Calls that reach a node plus the typed pass's edges) against the scip-go
call pairs whose callee the subject defines; the decision, every option's numbers and the revisit triggers are
design §1.6. O39/O40 are closed in fact: no selector-method or dispatch shape is a gap (DG-21), and what the
index sees and the served graph does not is a call through a func-typed field, variable or parameter and an
interface method no in-service type implements (O446). The Go row's L2 stayed unmet on precision (.7794) in wave le; wave lg made the graded set the product and it reads excellent (F.20).

- [x] Go's L2 grades the served calls: the typed pass's edges (goTypedEdges, in process) and the extracted nodes' Calls become `callgraph.BuildBoth` pairs, scored against the truth pairs whose callee the index defines; a dispatch edge per implementer collapses to one pair by method name, an unresolved function value and an instantiation give none and are counted, a call to a symbol the subject does not define leaves the truth and is counted, and goextract's pairs (option A) ride in the notes beside the graded set {id: LB-F176, symbol: scoreGoCalls, package: cmd/indexbench, reachable_from: TestCmdLangOracleGoTypedCalls, needs: "LB-F58,LB-F64"}
- [x] The SCIP scorer marks the truth pairs whose callee symbol the index defines in the subject (a document's definition or a local), so a harness can tell a call a service can serve from a call into the standard library or a dependency {id: LB-F177, symbol: ScoreSCIPWith, package: internal/langbench, reachable_from: TestScoreSCIPCallInIndex, needs: "LB-F64"}

### F.20 Go's served calls are the graded calls (wave lg, item go)

The owner-architect accepted design §1.6's decision (ADR-048 amendment 2026-10-02, decision (b)): Go's L2 grades the
calls the product serves. This section makes the graded set the product: E and D hold inside the served graph
(`callgraph.BuildBoth`, every `Build` plus `AddTypedEdges`, every package patch), with no store table, cache or job.
O475 records which surface reads which graph; symbol_context's lists move to the served graph. Go reads L2 excellent
(.9573 / .9219); LB-Q-go-rest carries the rest. Every row of the 90 plans reads the same before and after (2,694 rows, 355
failing; O478).

- [x] D: a syntax-only edge to a Go type or interface is a reference (`core.EdgeTypeRef`, in memory only, classified from the callee node's kind): `CallersDetail`, `CalleesDetail` and the L2 pairs leave it out, `ReferrersDetail`, `ReferencesDetail` and the dependents views return it labelled, and the reach walkers, `blast_radius` (`references`, by_depth `edge_kind`), review, the console and findings neighborhoods still read it; symbol_context answers from the served graph with `referenced_by` and `references` {id: LB-F200, symbol: Graph.edgeLabel, package: internal/analysis/callgraph, reachable_from: TestServedCalls_Golden, needs: "LB-F199"}
- [x] go.json is graded on the new served set (C, read through `CalleesDetail`) and its notes carry A, A resolved, B, the pre-lg C built explicitly, D, E and F, so every earlier option stays measurable {id: LB-F201, symbol: goCallPairSets, package: cmd/indexbench, reachable_from: TestCmdLangOracleGoTypedCalls, needs: "LB-F176,LB-F178,LB-F199,LB-F200"}

### F.23 CUE (wave fx, item cue)

CUE's first reading (LB-Q-cue, T6), against the cue binary itself: calibration/oracles/cue runs `cue lsp`
(document symbols, and its definition answer at every identifier) behind a `cue vet` gate, tier A, on cue-lang/cue's
own CI module (v0.16.1), monogon/NetMeta and this repository's schema/. The incumbent extractor read L1 unmet
(sym_recall .1278: a field inside a conjunction's, a list's or a comprehension's struct, or past six levels, was no
entity) and L2 unmet (call_recall .1751: only references to a #definition were recorded); the dialect reads L1 and L2
excellent. L3 and L4 are not attempted: a language's L3 is graded through the file resolver, which has no CUE import
strategy, and L4 through a SCIP index, which no CUE producer writes (LB-Q-cue-rest). CUE imports and cross-file
references reach ingest as doc-xref edges, which item timoni builds on.

- [x] The CUE dialect (internal/extractor/dialects/cue: native engine, `.cue`, kinds definition, field, package and import, the tool oracle's definition and field), the native registry's hook, and extractCUE as its DocResulter: a package entity per package clause (`package/<name>`, the file's root entity, apart from a field of the package's name), an import entity per string-literal import, `import 42` none {id: LB-F211, symbol: extractCUE, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCUEDialectGoldens}

### F.24 Timoni (wave fx, item timoni)

Timoni modules and bundles are plain CUE with a contract (`timoni.cue`, `values.cue`, `templates/*.cue`, `bundle.cue`,
`runtime.cue`), so the dialect is native and composes the CUE extraction: every CUE node and reference edge is the CUE
extractor's, unchanged, and the Timoni entities, calls and cross-file references ride on top. It edits neither
`cueextract.go` nor `typedcue.go` (item cue owns them); the composed extractor takes the CUE extractor's own
cross-file references when that extractor reports any. The oracle is the `timoni` CLI (offline: no cluster, no
registry pull), tier A for the cross-file references and tier C for definitions and references (a CUE syntax walk the
CLI gates, calibration/oracles/timoni); a bundle instance whose module is an `oci://` url needs a registry, so it has no truth and is counted
external (the subject's own `podinfo.cue`); the reading adds a bundle with `file://` instances to the scratch copy so
the bundle-to-module references have a subject.

- [x] A .cue file of a Timoni module (a timoni.cue, a file under a timoni.cue but never under cue.mod/, which holds the module's vendored dependencies), a bundle (a top-level `bundle:`) or a runtime (`runtime:` with a v1alpha1 apiVersion) is claimed by ingest and read through the Timoni extractor: classifyCUE tags it ToolTimoni, resolveExtractor and ReingestFile both call timoniDispatch, and every other .cue file reads as before, never gated out by the dialect's manifest (no sniff: a match.sniff on .cue would make every unmatched .cue ambiguous) {id: LB-F216, symbol: timoniDispatch, package: internal/fusion, reachable_from: TestResolveExtractor_Timoni, needs: "LB-F23"}
- [x] The Timoni extractor composes the CUE extractor and adds the Timoni entities (timoni/module, timoni/values, timoni/object/#Def for a definition with a literal Kubernetes kind, timoni/bundle, timoni/bundle/instance/<n>, timoni/runtime, timoni/runtime/cluster/<n>), the L2 calls (a struct-literal values: to #Config.<key>, a template that takes `#config: #Config` to #Config) and the label sets that join an object template to the #Instance that renders it {id: LB-F217, symbol: TimoniExtractor.ExtractDoc, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestTimoniDialectGoldens, needs: "LB-F216"}
- [x] A bundle instance's `module.url` of `file://<path>` is a repo-path reference to the module's timoni.cue (the path read from the bundle file's directory, as Timoni reads it), and #Instance is a label-selector reference, scoped by the module root, to each object template it names, so ingest stores the instance-to-module and instance-to-template edges; an oci:// url, a non-literal url, an absolute path and one that leaves the repository are counted by reason (TimoniUnresolvedRefs), never guessed {id: LB-F218, symbol: timoniWalk.moduleRef, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestTimoniBundleModuleRefs, needs: "LB-F217"}

### F.29 Dart to L4 (wave lx, item dart)

Dart read L3 good (file_edge_recall .3534) and L4 unmet (typed_recall .8464) at wave lb. The L3 miss was mostly the
oracle's: scip-dart numbers `local N` symbols per document, and the file-edge truth read them as global (577 of 1,194
edges), so the truth edge count falls to 617. The dialect's own share is Dart's `part` / `part of`: a library's parts
are its own names. L4 is S1 (LB-F239, item python), cherry-picked. What no dialect query reaches is filed, not built:
O533 (doc-comment references), O534 (a part reads its library's imports), O535 (lower case names, `show` names), O536
(typed receivers, a reference outside any definition), O537 (an import-prefix target in typed_recall).

The Rust dialect clears dispatch parity but for one oracle-blind file edge (O539), reads L4 good and import_resolution excellent without a resolver edit, and the harness leaves out what no source line writes. The switch itself is filed (O538), never made here (`internal/fusion/register.go` is wave lh's); each row about the dialect alone is a reading claim, a lexical contract on `rust.json`, never a claim that the product reaches it. The records are O538..O543.

### F.31 Ruby (wave lx, item ruby)

Ruby read L1 excellent and L2 and L3 good, each capped by its oracle's tier. Wave lx took the extraction first. A bare
identifier is now a call unless a local binds it: the tags engine's locals rule, the manifest's `queries.locals`
(LB-F254, LB-F255). Tier-C L2 on sinatra v4.2.1 + jekyll v4.4.1 moved from .8688/.9938 to .9944/.9942, every remaining
miss classified. Then wave lx measured both tier-A routes:

- scip-ruby on two Sorbet-typed gems indexed with their own sorbet/config (LB-F257, O545);
- a runtime require trace that Ruby itself evaluates (LB-F256, design §1.3, O547).

The re-pin rule was committed before measuring: take the typed gems only if their reading proves L3 and L1 excellent.
They lose L3 (file_edge_recall .0613 and .0794, the resolver's constant binding, O546), so sinatra and jekyll stay
graded and the typed numbers are the ceiling (LB-F258). Against the trace, the dialect reads L3 unmet on the graded
subjects (source roots the resolver lacks, O547). The graded L3 stays the tier-B dump's, and LB-Q-ruby-rest carries
both ceilings. Epoch need for the integrator: every `.rb`, `.rake`, `.ru` and Rakefile's calls change (bare-identifier
calls added), so Ruby needs the wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump. `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited
here.

- [x] A bare identifier (`helper`, `params` in `params.fetch`: no receiver, no arguments) is a Ruby call unless a local of that name was written at or before it in a scope it sees: the tags engine's locals rule reads `@local.scope` and `@local.definition` from the dialect's locals query in the same walk as the tags query, keeps a candidate of a pattern set `(#set! is-not "local")` only when no definition in its scope chain binds it (def, class, module, `class << self` and `def self.x` are gates; blocks and lambdas see out) and no other pattern names its node; every other dialect's output is byte-identical {id: LB-F254, symbol: keepNonLocalCalls, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestRubyLocalsRule}
- [x] A dialect manifest's `queries.locals` (dialects.go, schema/dialect with its valid and negative testdata) names the locals query, appended to the tags query at compile; a tags pattern set is-not "local" with no locals query, or a locals query no pattern uses, is refused at registry build {id: LB-F255, symbol: tsDialectRules.loadLocalsQuery, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestRubyLocalsManifest}
- [x] A runtime require trace (`calibration/oracles/ruby-require-trace`): each subject file loaded in its own child under the subject's own bundle and test load path, every require, require_relative, load and autoload that runs recorded with the file Ruby's lookup names, written as neutral imports, tier A when the executed literal statements are at least 90% of the in-repo ones (design §1.3); sinatra .9864, jekyll .9580, ruby-lsp .9793, spoom 1 {id: LB-F256, oracle: lexical, path: calibration/oracles/ruby-require-trace/ruby_require_trace.rb, pattern: "ratio >= 0.90", needs: "LB-F41"}

C# read L1 excellent, L2 unmet and L3 good at wave lh: scip-dotnet 0.2.14 writes a `.ctor` only at an attribute (O590),
no occurrence for a generic method called with type arguments or a delegate invoked (O271), and one target framework,
so 17.7% of fathom's call pairs sat where it could not judge, over the LB-F203 cap. Wave lf1 adds the C# compiler's own
semantic model as a tier-A calls and file-edge oracle beside it (LB-F287, O591, O592), reads scip-dotnet's references
at their written sites (LB-F286), and takes the dialect's real extraction further: constructions are calls (LB-F288),
fields, events, enum members, delegates and indexers are definitions and a property is named by its type, so a
same-named type binds again (LB-F289, O593, O595). The reading meets L4 with every level excellent. A Unity project is
measured at L1 and a MonoBehaviour's Unity messages are entrypoints (LB-F290). Epoch need for the integrator: every
`.cs` file's extraction changes (O594); `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. The records are O590..O595.

- [x] scip-dotnet's references are read in the dialect's names at their written site: `.ctor` and `.cctor` are the class, an indexer `this`, no arity suffix; a `.ctor` reference not written `new X(` (an attribute applied) is no call site and a type reference written `new X(` is a call to X, each counted and disclosed (9,000 and 12,130 on Polly and Newtonsoft.Json, O590) {id: LB-F286, symbol: csharpCallee, package: internal/langbench, reachable_from: TestScoreSCIPCSharpCallSites}

### F.33 C and C++ (wave lf1, item ccpp)

C++ is dispatched: the `cpp-macros` pre-pass read the decorator and try/catch macros the grammar misparsed (O455), lang-parity read MERGE against `cppextract.go`, and fusion serves C++ from its dialect (LB-F117). The include-path resolver then had to read the dialect's namespaced names (O596) and stop binding a member call by its receiver's name (O523, O597), and it follows an `#include` only where the build's preprocessor can keep it (O468). Both dialects name fields, enumerators and file- or namespace-scope variables (O367), and the harness reads scip-clang's L4 over what an extractor can name in the files a reading measures (O598) and leaves out, under LB-F203's cap, the file pairs the index cannot judge (O599). C reads L4 excellent at every level and C++ L1 and L2 excellent with L3 and L4 good, both level_met L4 (the code of fathom 085a1b86); C++'s CPU per byte sits at the 1 MB/s tenancy floor under load (O600), so its reading was taken on a calm machine. The records are O596..O602. Epoch need for the integrator: every `.cc .cpp .cxx .hpp .hh .hxx` file and every C++ `.h` (dispatch: namespaced names, macros, aliases, unions, members, receiver calls) and every `.c` and C `.h` (fields, enumerators, variables) is re-read, the wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here.

kind of typed edge that drew each (the 3,175 false pairs on cli/cli were interface-dispatch fan-out and instantiation facts,
not test files, build tags or generated code), Go's file-edge set is the edges that name their target (design §1.7, §1.2
amendment), and scip-go's package symbol and go-test testmains no longer count against the subject. The typed pass,
`goextract` and the served graph are unchanged (no `ExtractorEpoch` need): file_edge_precision .5625 to .9972,
file_edge_recall .6702 to .7034, L2 .9573 / .9219 unchanged, import_resolution 1. L4 reads def_mapping .5494 and
typed_recall .5805: struct fields (4,236 definitions, 28,084 references on cli/cli) and the per-file package symbol (728,
14,101) are the remainder, an owner decision (O606, O607); the field condition (a cross-language rule in ADR-048 or §1.4)
does not hold, so the extractor is left as it is.

wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here (O611). The records are O609..O612.

### F.37 Prometheus and PromQL (wave c1, item prom)

main-module dependency: the Prometheus parser checks it from the nested, test-only module `calibration/oracles/prometheus`

route-host value (design §4 "PromQL references"), so a rule's `job="checkout"` meets the `deploy:` key the checkout
Service or compose service provides; a regex or negative matcher is counted, never guessed. The records are O620..O624.

### F.38 The Kubernetes side pass and the OpenTelemetry Collector (wave c1, item mesh)

The route pass's Kubernetes side pass (routeref.go; the kubernetes dialect stays undispatched, its dispatch switch its own row)
reads what a mesh or a monitoring stack says about the objects beside it: Istio's gateways, subsets, egress entries and
authorization policies, prometheus-operator's monitors and probes, each joined to the Service, workload, Gateway,
DestinationRule or ServiceAccount it names and stored as a `route-ref` typed edge (file tier, never a gate, I57; no table).
A PrometheusRule's expressions are not joined: the Stage A static reading's kill fired (recall .583 under .60, every join
right, the rest platform or unlabelled; `docs/bench/readings/telemetry/stage-a-static.md`).
A container's telemetry identity and an OpenTelemetry Collector configuration (LB-Q-otel-collector) give TL-5 its
identities and Stage A its exporter routes. The records are O625..O629.

### F.39 Framework routes: the seam and Java (wave c2, item java)

The route seam (`docs/design/system-graph.md` §5 "Framework routes (wave c2)") makes `internal/analysis/routes` a
registry of framework readers. A reader reports a file's routes and its mounts. `Compose` applies the mounts across a
service's files, and the result reaches the `route:` key, `service_ref` hops and the capability inventory with no table
or column. Spring MVC and JAX-RS are the first readers past the legacy ones, measured against each running
application's own route dump (`docs/bench/readings/routes/java.md`). The records are O691..O693.

- [x] The route seam: framework readers register from `init()` (`Reader{Framework, Langs, Read}`, run in Framework order, a duplicate panics); `ExtractFile` runs the Go AST reader plus every reader of the file's language, sorted and deduplicated, and `Extract` stays its routes; `Compose` applies a reader's mounts across a service's files (`./x`, `../x`, `/x`, dotted modules, directories; recursive to depth 8, cycle-guarded; a file mounted N times yields N routes); a mount is stored as a `MOUNT` `service_routes` row and `RoutesFromRows` composes it away before `BuildFromStore` joins and before inventory keys `route:` items, so none reaches a hop, an item or a `route:` key; serviceref and inventory read the registry's languages, syslink's dialect map gains java and php, and the legacy JS/TS and Python pattern sets move unchanged into the `express-regex` and `python-regex` readers {id: LB-F321, symbol: Compose, package: internal/analysis/routes, reachable_from: TestBuildFromStore_ComposesMounts}
- [x] A Spring MVC controller's routes are read from `.java` source: `@RequestMapping`, `@GetMapping`, `@PostMapping`, `@PutMapping`, `@DeleteMapping` and `@PatchMapping` under the type's own `@RequestMapping` prefix (value or path, a string or an array; an empty or absent path is the prefix or the root; `method =` single or array, united with the type's, none is ANY); a String constant declared in the same file folds, any other reference drops the route; a `@FeignClient` type is a client; the handler is the java dialect's `Class.method` (`Outer.Inner.method`), so the route binds to its indexed JavaMethod {id: LB-F322, symbol: readSpring, package: internal/analysis/routes, reachable_from: TestSpringRoutes, needs: "LB-F321"}

### F.40 Framework routes: Node (wave c2, item node)

Node routes reach the route seam (item java, `internal/analysis/routes` registry and `Compose`) through four readers that
share one JS/TS token scanner (comments and strings are never tokens): Nest, Fastify, Express and Next.js. Each names a
route's handler as the TS/JS dialect names the definition (`Controller.method`, the function, `""` for an inline arrow), so a
route binds to its code, and reports the mounts no single file can resolve (an Express `use()` of a repo module, a Fastify
`register(module, {prefix})`, a Nest module tree under a global prefix). Measured by `indexbench route-oracle` against
tier-A oracles that read each framework's own route table (`calibration/oracles/{express,fastify,nest,nextjs}/`,
`docs/bench/readings/routes/node.md`): bound recall and precision 1.0 for Nest, Fastify and Next.js; Express .7632 / 1.0,
the ceiling below. The express-regex reader is retired (O696). Epoch need for the integrator: every `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs`,
`.cjs`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts` and `.cts` file's service_routes rows change (new readers, MOUNT rows, the regex's rows gone),
so the wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump must cover them; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. Seam
change requests (route: keys composed, Nest exclude and default version, recursive directory mounts) are O695. The records
are O694..O696.

- [x] Nest routes: a `@Controller(prefix | [prefixes] | {path, version})` class's `@Get`, `@Post`, `@Put`, `@Delete`, `@Patch`, `@All` (ANY), `@Head`, `@Options`, `@Search`, `@Sse` (GET) and WebDAV decorators, handler `Controller.method`; a version (`@Version`, else the controller's) as the URI segment `v{n}`, `VERSION_NEUTRAL` none; a `@Module`'s controllers and imported repo modules mounted under `""` or their `RouterModule.register` path (children joined), and `app.setGlobalPrefix` beside `NestFactory.create(Root)` mounting the root module, so the prefix reaches every controller down the module tree; nestjs/nest 2731c180 `sample/*` (29 apps, 78 routes): bound recall and precision 1.0, 82 of 82 handlers indexed {id: LB-F326, symbol: readNest, package: internal/analysis/routes, reachable_from: TestReadNest}
- [x] Fastify routes: `fastify.METHOD(path, [options,] handler)` (chained), `fastify.route({method, url, handler})`, an inline plugin's routes under its `register` prefix, `register` of a repo module a Mount under its prefix, `@fastify/autoload` a directory Mount under `options.prefix` and a route plugin under `routes/` read under its folders (dirNameRoutePrefix) and exported `autoPrefix`; fastify/demo 5cd56012 `src/` and fastify v5.12.5 `examples/` (9 apps, 26 routes): bound recall and precision 1.0 {id: LB-F327, symbol: readFastify, package: internal/analysis/routes, reachable_from: TestReadFastify}

### F.41 Framework routes: PHP and Python (wave c2, item phppy)

Five readers register at the route seam (F.39, LB-F321): Laravel and Symfony for PHP, Django with DRF, FastAPI and
Flask for Python. Before them fathom read no PHP route, and read Python routes with a line regex that bound none to
its handler and applied no prefix. Each reader parses the file (a small PHP and a small Python parser, nothing
executed), names a handler as the dialect names it (`Controller.method`, the function, `ViewSet.action`), and reports
group, import, include, router and blueprint mounts for `Compose`. They are measured against each running framework's
own route table (`docs/bench/readings/routes/php.md`, `python.md`): Laravel, FastAPI and Flask read PASS; Symfony and
Django read every route their files declare and carry counted ceilings. The python-regex reader yields to the Flask and
FastAPI readers on the files they read. Epoch need for the integrator: the `service_routes` rows of every `.php` and
`.py` file a reader reads, and of `config/routes` YAML, change (routes and MOUNT rows added, python-regex rows replaced),
so the wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump must cover them; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. Two seam
tests assert the pre-item behaviour this item changes by design (O697). The records are O697..O699.

### F.42 Doc-engine value predicates (wave c2, item engine)

K20 (owner, 2026-10-09): value predicates in the core document engine, universal (K15) and opt-in, so a dialect that
sets none extracts byte for byte as before. A key-absent selector, an anchored RE2 value predicate, a k=v split of a
string value and template refusal for identity captures (with one entity per name per file) lift TL-9's ceilings (the
Compose list form, `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES`, an interpolated identity), move GitHub Actions' pin classification into
data so its wrapper goes, and make an id'd run step one entity (O631). Design `docs/design/language-breadth.md` §4.
Records: O631 (closed), O703..O705.

### F.43 Go to L4 (wave lf2, item go)

K17, K22 and K18's Go half (owner-architect, 2026-10-09). Every automatic go invocation runs the local toolchain (K22).
A Go package is a counted namespace in L4, never a symbol (K18): def_mapping .5494 to .5891, typed_recall .5805 to .6713.
K17's member pass (struct fields `Owner.name`, interface methods `Iface.Method`, every field reference a typed edge) was
built and read L4 excellent (def_mapping .9632, typed_recall .9957), then failed its own cost gate, also with an exported
struct's fields only, and is reverted: Go stays L3, L4 unmet on the field class alone, the owner's call. Design
`docs/design/language-breadth.md` §1.2, §1.4 (amendments 2026-10-10), §1.7 (trigger fired). Records: O607 and O654 point 3
(closed), O606 (open, K17 measured), O672..O676. No `ExtractorEpoch` need: the extraction and the typed edges ingest stores
are unchanged on main.

### F.44 Python's module symbol (wave lf2, item python)

K18 (owner-architect, 2026-10-09; ADR-048 amendment 2026-10-10): a file whose top level executes code gets one minted
per-file module symbol, the FROM of its top-level calls and imports and the target of an import of it; a pure
namespace is file granularity. Built as a tags-engine capability a manifest turns on (`module_symbol`), so JS/TS, Ruby,
Lua and shell need no engine work; python turns it on here. scip-python defines one `<path>/__init__:` per document
(1,258 on fastapi + flask), so every `.py` file qualifies, an empty `__init__.py` included. The symbol is `<module>`
(`core.ModuleSymbolName`), which never collides with or joins a file's own `X.__init__` (O527: 56 mis-joins, 41 by the
leaf fallback and 15 by position, and 214 references made from them). Design `docs/design/language-breadth.md` §2.2 and
§3 item 8. Epoch need for the integrator: every `.py` file's symbols change (one module symbol added, with the
module-level calls) and its file-resolved edges (module imports), so the wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump must cover
them; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. Records: O527 (closed), O677..O680.

- [x] The dialect manifest's `module_symbol` field: the key of a kind of the manifest, canonical `module`, neither container nor callable, outside `oracle_kinds`, on the tags engine with the `dir` package rule; `schema/dialect` #Dialect validates it with valid and invalid testdata (negatives 16c..16h, TestDialectModuleSymbolSchema) and LoadDialects refuses the same manifests at load {id: LB-F346, symbol: checkModuleSymbol, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestModuleSymbolRefusedAtLoad}
- [x] The tags engine's module pass: with the manifest's switch on, each file (never an injected range) gets one `<module>` definition of the switch's kind, line 1 to its last line, owning the calls no definition encloses or a call attachment claims; every dialect without the switch compiles without it and extracts byte-identically to its goldens, conan on python's grammar included {id: LB-F347, symbol: mintTSModule, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestModuleSymbolOnlyWhereTheManifestNamesIt, needs: "LB-F346"}
- [x] Python turns the module symbol on (`PythonModule`, canonical `module`, outside `oracle_kinds`; its goldens change by additions only): the file resolver makes top-level references and imports from it and an import naming a module (`import a.b`, the module a `from` import reads, a submodule it names) a file-resolved edge to that module's symbol, the symbol no top-level name of its file; `internal/scip` maps a scip-python module definition to it and to nothing else (no leaf or position join), so `fathom ingest --scip` makes top-level references from it and an import's module reference targets it {id: LB-F348, symbol: moduleImportEdges, package: internal/fusion, reachable_from: TestResolveFileEdges_PythonModuleSymbol, needs: "LB-F347"}
- [x] (op) LB-F349: the Python reading re-measured before (origin/main 784657b9, reproducing the committed reading exactly) and after on fastapi 0.119.1 + flask 3.1.3, universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) and scip-python 0.6.6 (tier A) in each subject's dependency environment: def_mapping .8906 to .9985, typed_recall .952 to .9979, L4 good to excellent; file_edge_recall .5119 to .9191, file_edge_precision .9918 to .9875, call_precision .9179 to .9184, call_recall .9936 to .9922, import_resolution .951 and L1 unchanged; O527's 56 mis-joined module definitions and 214 references made from them to 0 {id: LB-F349, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/python.json, pattern: "\"fathom:PythonModule\": 1258", needs: "LB-F348"}

### F.45 Framework routes: composition (wave lf2, item routes2)

Wave c2's route seam follow-ups (O691, O693, O695, O698). A Mount can name a directory tree, so Symfony's
recursive controller import and @fastify/autoload's folder prefixes compose through the seam, and the
Fastify reader's in-file copy of autoload's folder rule is gone. Every consumer of `route:` keys reads a
service's composed routes: service_ref hops, inventory items, and syslink's `route:` provides rows,
which had keyed one file's local paths. O691 is decided by deletion: `DefaultRegistry` had only test
callers and no row, a per-file route source cannot compose, and SG-10, the entry point that will own a
registry, needs SG-2's `contract_keys` table, frozen until RT-M1 reads PASS, so nothing is wired here;
`routes.Extract` lost its last caller and goes with it. Nest's `exclude` and `defaultVersion` are not
built: no subject has a route they would fix (O695). No table, column, cache or job; every new Target
is stored in the existing MOUNT `service_routes` row. Epoch need for the integrator: the MOUNT rows of a
Symfony `config/routes` YAML with a directory resource or `routing.controllers` (`/src/Controller/`
becomes `/src/Controller/**`) and of a file registering `@fastify/autoload` (`./routes/` becomes
`./routes/**?dirs`), and the route rows of every Fastify plugin under a `routes/` directory (its folders
leave its local paths); `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. Deadcode over
`./cmd/...`: 516 before, 510 after. Records: O682 (new), the status lines of O691, O693, O695 and O698.

### F.46 The tracker's dispatch column (wave lf2, item langdoc)

K23 (owner, 2026-10-09): `docs/languages.md` cannot misstate which extractor handles a language. The template typed its
"Extraction" column by hand and went stale: Java, C++ and PHP read "heuristic ... dispatched" long after lf1 moved them to
the tags dialects. The column is now "Engine / dispatch", generated from fusion's own registry (`fusion.DispatchTable`:
the per-key answer of what `New` registers, plus sniff-only and refused dialects) through one placeholder per row; the
prose beside a placeholder keeps only what the registry cannot know (grammar pins, oracle notes, refusal reasons). No
extractor, dispatch or ExtractorEpoch change: docs and tooling only. Records: O687, O688.

### F.47 Alertmanager and Grafana (wave c3, item obs)

K14/K15/K19/K20 (owner, 2026-10-09; synthesis rows 13 and 14): the alerting and dashboard halves of observability as
code join the Prometheus rules they act on, through the one PromQL scanner (`internal/promql`), and never through a
`deploy:` label join (K19: the Stage A kill stays fired). An Alertmanager configuration's route tree is evaluated under
each alerting rule's name and static labels, with Alertmanager's own semantics, so a rule reaches the receivers its
alerts would; a matcher on a label the rule does not set statically makes that edge a `may`, never a certainty. A
Grafana panel's query is read as PromQL only when its datasource is Prometheus, its macros substituted and its
template variables refused as names. No secret-bearing field is selected or stored. Design
`docs/design/language-breadth.md` §4 "Observability documents". Records: O716..O720.

### F.48 CloudWatch as IaC and Lambda handlers (wave c3, item cw)

Synthesis row 12 (proposed there as LB-F308, never minted; LB-F308 stays unused): what an alarm watches and
notifies, what a metric filter counts and reads, and what code a Lambda function runs, in CloudFormation and SAM
templates and in Terraform. Data before code (K20): the cloudformation dialect declares a metric filter's metric
as an identity entity and reads an alarm's Sub-written Dimensions and actions as references; a new `identity`
resolve strategy carries a `key=value` identity signature (TL-5's form), joined to the entities signed with it,
nearest first, across files and dialects. The handler binding is code (SAM's Globals defaults and each runtime's
handler rule are no selector), one line of the doc-xref pass. Every edge is a file-tier doc-xref typed edge (I57:
never a gate; no table). Design `docs/design/language-breadth.md` §4. Records: O721..O725 (O723 closed).

### F.50 React components and routes (wave c3, item react)

K14: React's own constructs become symbols and edges of the typescript and javascript dialects, and React
Router's routes reach the route seam. A component is a PascalCase function, or module-scope function binding,
whose body writes JSX, a PascalCase binding to memo, forwardRef, lazy, a styled template or a PascalCase member,
or a class extending Component or PureComponent; a hook is a use[A-Z0-9] function that calls a hook; a prop is a
member of a *Props interface or type alias, named after it; a JSX element whose tag is PascalCase or a member is
a call to that tag. The kinds sit outside `oracle_kinds` (Python's `field` precedent) with canonical kinds
function, class, function and field, so the TS/JS grading of every existing definition is unchanged. Measured by
`indexbench lang-react` against react-docgen-typescript with the TypeScript checker (tier C) and by
`route-oracle` against React Router's own route objects (tier A); step 0 and the ceilings are in LB-Q-react-rest.
Epoch need for the integrator: every `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.cts`, `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs` and `.cjs` file's
symbols (a refined kind, a new component binding, a prop, a type alias's method now `Alias.method`) and calls (a
JSX element) change, and a React Router file's `service_routes` rows; seed file
`internal/extractor/dialects/typescript/testdata/react.tsx`. `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited
here. Records: O731..O735.

- [x] The typescript and javascript dialects' React kinds: JSComponent (a PascalCase function or module-scope function binding whose body writes JSX, never a CONSTANT_CASE name; a PascalCase binding to memo, forwardRef, lazy, a styled template or a PascalCase member), JSClassComponent (a class extending Component or PureComponent), JSHook (a use[A-Z0-9] function that calls a hook) and, in TypeScript, JSProp (a member of a *Props interface or type alias, its object type read directly or one or two intersections deep, named Owner.prop; the type alias kind becomes a container); a JSX element with a PascalCase or member tag is a call from its enclosing definition, an intrinsic tag none; canonical kinds function, class, function and field, outside oracle_kinds; an ingest stores the kinds and draws a JSX element's and a hook call's file-resolved edge, same-file and imported {id: LB-F377, symbol: KindJSComponent, package: internal/core, reachable_from: TestIngest_ReactComponents}

### F.51 TypeScript and JavaScript to L4 (wave lf3, item tsjs)

K18 for TypeScript and JavaScript, then the residue O746's table measured, cheapest first under K17's cost gate
(zod, median of three alternating ingests against the previous commit: +20% store, +15% wall at most). Step 1
(O746, before any code): 85.8% of TypeScript's unmapped typed references were made from the file module, 44% of its
def_mapping denominator is object-literal properties. L3 reaches excellent on both dialects; L4 does not: def_mapping
is capped by object-literal properties, which no cost gate admits (O747), so LB-Q-typescript and LB-Q-javascript stay
open with their -rest rows rewritten. The module symbol itself costs +33% store on zod and +37% on nest against
origin/main (O748, the wave's mandated step; the gate was applied to the steps after it). Epoch need for the
integrator: every `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.cts`, `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs` and `.cjs` file gains `<module>` (JSModule), a
JSConst per module-level const binding, and in TypeScript a JSField per class field, interface or object type alias
property, enum member and parameter property (JavaScript: class fields); top-level calls move to `<module>`, a
const's initializer calls and a field's to it; module imports and barrel re-exports become module edges; a CommonJS
`module.exports.x =` or `exports.x =` of a function or a wrapped one is a JSFunc (O721); a `.ts` file's PascalCase
function whose body text holds `</` is a function, no longer a component. Seed files
`internal/extractor/dialects/typescript/testdata/shapes.ts` and `internal/extractor/dialects/javascript/testdata/common.cjs`.
`internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. Records: O746..O749; O721, O732, O505, O275 fixed.

### F.52 Rust on the tags engine (wave lf3, item rust)

K25 (owner-architect, 2026-10-10): ruling 3 (O539) resolves a SCIP symbol defined in more than one document to every
defining document in the harness, a counted rule; ruling 4 (O541, K18) makes a reference to a Rust module path a FILE
edge to the module's defining file, never a symbol, and K18 makes a Rust `mod` a namespace whose descriptors leave L4,
counted. With both, lang-parity reads MERGE and `.rs` is dispatched to the tags dialect (O538's replacement), the CGO
walker is retired and the reading reads every level excellent. Ruling 3 read literally for every symbol collapses Rust's
file-edge truth (rust-analyzer gives every crate target of a package one `crate/`; file_edge_recall .0583), so
rust-analyzer's own per-target copies of one symbol are narrowed, counted and flagged for the owner (O751). Epoch need
for the integrator: every `.rs` file's symbols change (RustConst, RustStatic, more structs and fields, an impl named by
its head type, no constructor call) and its file-resolved edges (a module path's file edge), so the wave's one
`ExtractorEpoch` bump must cover them; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. Records: O751..O755
(O538..O542 closed or updated).

- [x] A SCIP symbol defined in more than one document resolves to every defining document in the file-edge truth (K25 ruling 3, O539): the pairs only a later definer draws are counted (`scip_file_edges_every_definer`: 311 on ripgrep and clap) and disclosed in each subject's notes; scip-go's package (LB-F298) and C#'s multiply-defined symbol (LB-F287) keep their own rules; rust-analyzer's per-target copies of one symbol, which it reports as "Duplicate symbol" itself, are narrowed and counted (`scip_file_edges_duplicate_narrowed`: 12,075): a crate written by its package's name is the library root, a reference inside a defining document is to its own copy, a module is the copy in the nearest directory above the reference (O751) {id: LB-F387, symbol: definerEdges.add, package: internal/langbench, reachable_from: TestScoreSCIPEveryDefiningDocument}

### F.53 The CGO walkers retired (wave lf3, item cgo)

K25 (owner, 2026-10-10): every CGO walker fusion no longer dispatches leaves the tree, and LB-F19's precondition is
the import, not each language's L4 target (ruling 1). `.sh` and `.bash` move to the bash tags dialect at lang-parity
MERGE (L2, against the reading's own ctags and tier-C tags oracles); `.h`'s dispatch cell names the c and cpp dialects
the CT-3 router has picked between since LB-F119 and LB-F117, and the CGO router over cextract.go and cppextract.go,
which only lang-parity built, leaves after reading MERGE on the headers; the nine walkers no key dispatches are
deleted, their uses turned into dialect goldens or dropped where a committed parity reading proves the comparison;
the one-parse contract moves to a CGO-free file. After this item the go-tree-sitter importers are
`internal/extractor/treesitter/`, `nativespecs.go` and its probe test, `rustextract.go` and `scalaextract.go`; the
rust and scala items retire theirs in parallel, and treesitter/ goes in h3 with LB-F19. Epoch need for the
integrator: every `.sh` and `.bash` file (parity's SourceKind diff: BashVariable gained, one per `export NAME=value`,
none lost; on the two subjects' 58 files the walker's 309 BashFunctions keep their names and lines and gain an end

added: 1); none for `.h` (the label only) or for the deleted walkers (none was dispatched). `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is

it match nothing, so its pattern is now the character-class form these rows use).

### F.54 Scala on the tags engine (wave lf3, item scala)

`.scala` and `.sc` move from the CGO walker `scalaextract.go` to the scala tags dialect on tree-sitter-scala v0.26.2 (design §11,
dispatch at parity, ADR-048 D1). universal-ctags 6.2.1 has no Scala parser and scip-java needs an sbt build, so the oracle is a
scalameta syntax dump (tier B, definitions and calls); the grammar's upstream `tags.scm` (tier C) is a disclosed calls cross-check
that grades no member call (O763). The reading is L2 (L1 good, L2 good): every metric reads excellent but range_agreement, the
engine's end line on this grammar's outdent (O761); L3 and L4 stay open on LB-Q-scala, which keeps its L4 contract, with
LB-Q-scala-rest naming the ceiling. Epoch need for the integrator (O762): every `.scala` and `.sc` file's extraction changes, the
SourceKind diff from lang-parity being ScalaConstructor, ScalaType, ScalaVal and ScalaVar gained on the subjects (ScalaEnum,
ScalaEnumCase and ScalaGiven too, on Scala 3 sources) and none lost, with renamed nested containers and their methods, backticked
names without backticks, a Signature on every def and calls by the callee's last identifier; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go`
is not edited here. The tree-sitter-scala Go module stays required in `go.mod` with no importer, for the integrator to drop.
Records: O761..O764.

Kotlin's file_edge_precision read .7993 against the kotlin-psi import dump: O804 classes the 1,646 fathom-only edges
(1,593 same-package pairs, 53 one library import) and O521 left 269 `Class.Companion.member` imports unresolved. The
resolver half is fixed in the namespace-dir member import (LB-F431, LB-F432); the same-package half belongs to the
truth (K19, O519): LB-F433 was committed alone, then kotlin.json was re-read (L3 good) and a holdout read once. C++'s
617 undrawn file pairs (O805) were mostly an include or a file-level macro with no definition to draw from: a C++
file is now a module (LB-F434, LB-F435); cpp.json is not re-read, because K25 ruling 3, applied to scip-clang after
the reading, reads every namespace reopening as a definer (O806, an owner ruling). Scala gains imports and package
clauses (LB-F438) and an import truth committed first (LB-F439); its one L3 reading resolves imports but draws few
file edges (LB-F440, O807). Epoch needs for the integrator (`internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here):
Kotlin's stored file-resolved edges (resolver only, no extraction change); every C++ file's extraction (its module
symbol) and file-resolved edges; every `.scala` and `.sc` file's extraction (Package from the clauses, imports) and
file-resolved edges. Records: O804..O807 (O808..O810 unused).

## Q. The queue — one row per `language-breadth.queue.json` entry, in queue order

Each row's contract is its reading's computed `level_met` (`L[234]` accepts the target or
above). The row title names the engine, grammar source and licence, the oracle(s), and the
incumbent it retires. Workers follow design §13; the needs list is the queue entry's
`depends_on`.

### Wave 1 — the most-used languages, the web gap, the first tool formats

- [ ] TypeScript (.ts .tsx .mts .cts) — language at L4 (tags engine; tree-sitter/tree-sitter-typescript, MIT; oracle: ctags, scip-typescript) {id: LB-Q-typescript, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/typescript.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L4\"", needs: "LB-F1,LB-F2,LB-F3,LB-F4,LB-F12,LB-F13,LB-F15,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F20,LB-F21,LB-F22,LB-F5,LB-F7,LB-F11"}

L3's 1,882 missed file pairs and the 32 unresolved imports (O506), L2's 926 fathom-only pairs (O443's locals) and L1's 123 dialect-only definitions (O266) are not re-classified this wave", trigger: "the owner's decision on object-literal properties (O747: def_mapping leaves scip-typescript's per-file object-literal symbols out with a counted disclosure, as LB-F239 leaves parameters, or a definition per object-literal property ships under a cheaper store shape), then L4 re-read on nest + zod; O749's scope rule for the tsx documents"}

L2 stays excellent at call_recall .9383: the module is a caller, and scip-typescript writes a call at an object-literal method's own definition (`get() {` in an Object.defineProperty descriptor) that module-level code holds, which no extractor of call sites writes. React Router through `route-oracle` against React Router's own route objects (calibration/oracles/react-router, tier A): react-router 6.30.6's 22 example apps bound recall .9794 (95 of 97), bound precision .9596 (95 of 99), 20 of 22 PASS; with bulletproof-react's v7 router, pooled .8879 / .95 {id: LB-Q-react-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "Every level is good, not excellent: the L1 oracle is tier C (docgen's component test is a type heuristic: any function of one parameter), which caps each level at good (design §1.3), and the edges are graded over its population. The residue under that cap: L1's 16 misses are 3 props written as method signatures (onClose(): void), which stay methods named Owner.method (one node is one definition and the method pattern names every method signature), 9 props declared in an unnamed type literal on a parameter or a type argument and 2 in a union inside an intersection, which have no *Props owner to be named after (O733), and 1 class extending Next's Document; L2 and L3 have no miss since the TS/JS module symbol (LB-F381) made module-level code (story objects' render functions, describe and test callbacks, main.tsx's render) a From (O275 fixed). Routes: bulletproof-react names every path through an imported constant (`paths.app.users.path`), which a per-file reader cannot resolve (1 of its 9 paths read, the splat, and none bound); a React.lazy element is named by its own binding (About) where React Router renders the module's function (AboutPage); a lazy module exporting `element` instead of `Component` is unnamed; and the Next.js reader reads a Vite app's `src/pages/*.tsx` as Next pages (lazy-loading: 2 extra routes, O734)", trigger: "a tier-A or tier-B React component oracle (the TypeScript checker's own JSX element-type test, or React's compiler), for excellent; an engine that stores a definition's reference to a type in its own file and per-variant queries (O732, O733), for inline props and a component-to-props edge; a route seam that carries a lazy route's module and project-level gating for the Next.js reader (O734, O735)"}

- [-] Dockerfile at L2 excellent, below its L3 target: sym_recall .9949, sym_precision .9975, ref_recall 1.0, ref_precision 1.0 against a tier-A oracle (buildkit-dockerfile-parser — the parser `docker buildx build --print=outline` itself is built on; no Docker daemon is reachable in this sandbox to run buildx directly, hadolint's own JSON output is lint findings, not definitions) over 3 real subjects/73 files, docker/cli d1c06ef + docker/compose a32dc3d + docker-library/python 8f2cb2e, 2026-09-28; universal-ctags 6.2.1 has no Dockerfile parser (tier B/C both unavailable) {id: LB-Q-dockerfile-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L3 is not measurable this wave. The edges exist: since LB-X-docker-image-ref an ingest reads each Dockerfile as its stages (DockerfileDocFile, from this dialect's own nodes) and stores FROM and COPY --from of an image the repository builds (a compose image+build pair, a docker-bake target, a literal docker build -t in a script, Makefile or CI step) as doc-xref edges to the root stage of that Dockerfile. The harness cannot score them: scoreXrefOracle feeds fusion.ResolveDocXrefs only the measured dialect's own files, so no build site is in its set, and a tags-engine tool format reaches xref only through an import strategy (LB-F56, scoreFileXrefOracle), which a Dockerfile has none of; both are the harness's (langoracle_xref.go, resolvestrategy.go). Ceilings the reading would also show: a templated FROM (${BASE}) or tag is never joined, a tag two Dockerfiles build joins nothing, and only Dockerfile and *.dockerfile are dispatched, so a build naming Dockerfile.prod or a Containerfile resolves to nothing", trigger: "the harness feeds ResolveDocXrefs the same doc-candidate set an ingest feeds (O165's second fix) for a tags-engine tool format with a stand-alone DocResulter (fusion.DockerfileDocFile); then dockerfile.json is re-measured with a buildkit-parse oracle (moby/buildkit frontend/dockerfile/parser, tier A for FROM and stages) whose cross_references join each FROM and COPY --from to the in-repo build site that builds the image, on a subject whose Dockerfiles build FROM one another"}

On the old truth 325 of its 1,093 pairs stay undrawn after LB-F434, from O805's classes of the 617 before it: a reopened namespace's first definer (195, the truth's), a header's declaration to its out-of-line definition in an -inl.h or .cc the includes never reach and one test file to another (148, O469), names of reached headers crefs does not bind (97), the include lines (109) and file-level macros (68) LB-F434 draws. L4 good, not excellent: def_mapping .8989 vs .95: 590 definitions unmapped: special members the compiler writes or a class defaults (`operator=`, `~iter_impl`, `= default` constructors, which the dialect does not tag), conversion and literal operators, using-declarations scip-clang defines as aliases (`using testing::Invoke;`), constants local to a macro-written test body (gtest TestBody, Catch2 CATCH2_INTERNAL_TEST_n), data members behind a macro the pre-pass does not read and json's basic_json behind NLOHMANN_BASIC_JSON_TPL_DECLARATION; 7,146 macro-written definitions, 13,516 out of scope (fmt's bundled gtest, json's unit tests) and the file and nameless ones are counted apart (LB-F292, LB-F293); typed_recall .9656 is excellent. L2 call_recall .9292: the calls written in a macro's body joined the truth with their callers mapped (O601). Tenancy: the cpp-macros pre-pass costs about 1.8x the dialect's CPU per byte (an error query on every file, a second parse of 138 of 232, 51 of which still do not parse), which leaves the dialect at the 1 MB/s floor on a loaded machine (O600); LB-M5 reads FAIL at 1.92x (footprint.md, 2026-10-02)", trigger: "the owner's ruling on O806's scip-clang truth rule, then cpp.json re-read with LB-F434 (L3); a pre-pass shape for a template-declaration macro and Catch2's TEST_CASE with string arguments (O600), symbols for a class's defaulted special members and using-declarations, a reference kept where the pre-pass blanks a macro, and an O(1) root error check in the tags engine (O600); then cpp.json is re-read"}

an ingest resolves them through LB-X-docker-image-ref) and includes or extends of fragments no compose name rule claims (common.yaml, apps/*.yaml), which an ingest reads by content and resolves but the harness, owning files by name (ownedSourceRefs), drops; the root entity ComposeFile is outside the L1 kind intersection because the tool names no file entity; networks, volumes, secrets and configs are not entities (the selector engine drops null-valued map entries, and SymbolKey carries no kind, so a volume named like its service would collapse into one symbol), extends between services is no Calls edge (config merges and erases it), and the doc decoder does not apply YAML merge keys", trigger: "the harness owns compose fragments by content as ingest does (ownedSourceRefs) and a subject with extends.file between compose files is added, then docker-compose.json is re-measured; property expansion is not modelled, so a ProjectReference written with $(RepoRoot) or $(MSBuildThisFileDirectory) resolves to nothing (2 of 38 oracle edges) and no <Import> edge is drawn at all (Polly and aspnetcore write theirs with a property), a declaration under a false condition is still a reference (the oracle unions the Debug and Release evaluations of every framework the tool lists, so a framework a macOS run cannot evaluate stays out of its truth), a Directory.Build.props is imported implicitly and its items belong to the importing project for the tool and to the props file here, aspnetcore's <Reference Include=assembly> items that eng/targets/ResolveReferences.targets turns into ProjectReferences (12 oracle edges, attributed to that targets file, outside src/Http) are not modelled, and the central package versions (PackageVersion, GlobalPackageReference), Choose/When blocks, items inside a target, properties and a target's own DependsOnTargets, BeforeTargets and AfterTargets are not entities or references; aspnetcore cannot be evaluated from a clean checkout (its root Directory.Build.props and .targets import artifacts/bin/GenerateFiles/*, which its own first build step writes), so the oracle run stubs both as empty projects", trigger: "a solution decoder in the doc engine (or a root-element selector for .slnx) and a property-expanding value for ProjectReference and Import; then .sln joins the oracle through dotnet sln list, Import edges join the truth and the reading is re-measured"}

### Wave 3 — the next tier, components and the build/CI family

- [x] Kustomize (kustomization.yaml) — tool format at L3 (doc engine; pure-go:gopkg.in/yaml.v3, n/a; oracle: kustomize build (tier A); retires kustomizeextract.go) {id: LB-Q-kustomize, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/kustomize.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[34]\"", needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F21,LB-F22,LB-F18,LB-Q-kubernetes"}
- [-] Kustomize beyond the base and overlay directories an ingest resolves today: measured against Kustomize v5.8.1 (kubectl v1.36.1's embedded build; tier A, definitions, references and cross-file references, each entry kept only when the build changes without it) on kubernetes-sigs/kustomize kustomize/v5.8.1 (examples: 18 kustomizations, 16 built by the tool) and argoproj/argocd-example-apps 8088f4c (4 files, 3 built), 2026-09-29: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (89 definitions), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (70 entries, every one evidenced by the tool), xref_precision and xref_recall 1 (13 of 13 edges), so L1, L2 and L3 excellent; a scratch ingest of both subjects stores the same 13 edges {id: LB-Q-kustomize-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "a directory a resource or base names resolves to the one doc file in it (design section 4), and that holds today only because an ingest dispatches a Kubernetes manifest to the incumbent K8sExtractor, which is not a doc-engine extractor: the day the kubernetes dialect is dispatched every base that holds a manifest holds two doc files and its overlay edge stops resolving (TestKustomizeBaseDirectoryNeedsTheOneFileRule pins the rule); a resource that names a manifest is recorded but resolves to nothing because no manifest has a root entity in the store yet, and the harness scores only edges between the files the dialect owns (53 oracle edges to manifests are out of scope); a remote base or patch names no path of the repository (loadHttp is left out of the measure, and no build offline can follow it); the tool cannot build 3 of the 22 kustomizations (a remote base, kustomizeconfig/mykind.yaml and all.yaml are not in the checkouts) so they are not measured; transformers, generators, validators, configurations, crds, replacements, replicas and helmCharts are not entities or references; a patch is signed by its target name and an image by its newName because the doc engine has no concatenation; the measure is small (19 files, 13 base or overlay edges) and both subjects are documentation and demonstration trees", trigger: "fusion.ResolveDocXrefs preferring the file the referring dialect owns when a directory holds several doc files (needed before the kubernetes dialect is dispatched) and a manifest root entity that a resource entry can resolve to; then the reading is re-measured on a larger subject with components, transformers and overlays of overlays"}

Measured 2026-09-29 by parsing every calibration file with the grammar and counting those whose tree holds an ERROR or MISSING node (calibration/corpus.d/assembly.yaml: cfenollosa/os-tutorial master 8002382, 71 NASM .asm files; BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3 1.8.7 c/, 8 GAS .S and 4 MASM .asm files; universal-ctags 6.2.1 reads all three syntaxes with one parser, so the L1 oracle was never the obstacle) {id: LB-Q-assembly-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real assembly under the 5% line: 69 of the 83 calibration files (83.1%: os-tutorial 57 of 71, BLAKE3 12 of 12) parse with an error under RubixDev/tree-sitter-asm v0.24.0 (5bb5b03) and under upstream main 839741f (2025-11-08, six commits later and the best pin measured, which changed neither count); it is one generic grammar and lacks the common shapes of all three dialects: GAS directives with arguments (.p2align 6, .section .note.GNU-stack,'',%progbits, .type f, @function, .equ N, 8), NASM [org 0x7c00], %include, $ and $$, quoted data (db 'hi', 0), MASM quoted segment classes ('CODE'). Re-plan when a tree-sitter grammar (or one per dialect: GAS, NASM, MASM) parses the calibration subjects with at most 5% of files in error: pin it, then build the dialect (global labels and macros, local labels .L* and numeric 1: excluded, universal-ctags Asm kinds define, label, macro, type through oracle_kinds), dispatch .s .S .asm (an ExtractorEpoch bump) and measure L1 at tier B"}

- [-] MATLAB below L1, and dispatched: a `.m` file whose first KiB opens with a function, a classdef or a % comment is read by the tree-sitter dialect (acristoffers/tree-sitter-matlab v1.3.1, MIT: classes, properties, methods and functions, each callable with a signature and range; 8 of 3,180 calibration files parse with an error) and an Objective-C `.m` is never claimed; no level is claimed because universal-ctags 6.2.1 cannot prove L1 for it {id: LB-Q-matlab-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "blocked on an oracle (design §14.2): ctags 6.2.1 reports MatLab classes, functions and variables only, so a method is an unscoped function and a property is a variable among 45,154 tagged assignments of chebfun v5.7.0 and export_fig v3.58 (3,179 files); required_kinds keeps class, function, method and property whole, method and property fall outside the kind intersection, and lang-oracle refuses the reading (design §1.4: required kind method is outside the kind intersection). For the record, on an uncommitted scratch manifest that compares classes and functions only (no reading, no level): sym_recall .8758, sym_precision .9907, kind_agreement 1, signature_coverage 1, 2.45 MB/CPU-s, 433 peak module pages, 0 faults (dialect notes: internal/extractor/dialects/matlab/PROVENANCE.md); ctags also misses 12 classes and writes no MatLab end line, so range_agreement would cap L1 at good even with an oracle for the kinds", trigger: "an L1 oracle that reports the methods and properties of a classdef (a MATLAB parser dump or a SCIP producer): measure the four required kinds against it, then flip LB-Q-matlab; L2 needs a call oracle as well"}

- [-] Prometheus beyond the static reading: the label-matcher half of Stage A measured dynamically, the http_route half, a repository's own identifying labels, and VictoriaMetrics {id: LB-Q-prometheus-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the static reading (calibration/oracles/prometheus, promtool 3.15.0) grades a matcher's join only where a corpus declares the service it names, and none of the measured rule files does: every identifying-label value of thanos, node_exporter and kube-prometheus' extracted specs is undeclared or external on both sides (kube-prometheus' namespaced matchers meet its Services through the kubernetes side pass, item mesh), so the Stage A label join's recall >= .80 and precision >= .95 against a running app's /api/v1/targets and /api/v1/series (opentelemetry-demo, kube-prometheus) is unmeasured; the http_route to route: half needs wave C2's framework routes; the identifying label set is the universal default (promql.IdentityLabels), with no per-repository override until ADR-061's .fathom/ zones; MetricsQL and vmalert are not read. Ceilings, disclosed in the dialect's PROVENANCE.md: a .rules file (dockprom's alert.rules) is no YAML-routed extension; a rule_files glob resolves to nothing at ingest (fusion's doc-xref resolver matches globs to directories only); a rule file whose groups hold no rule is not claimed by content (promtool/prometheus-config.rules.good.yml); a file claimed by content alone reaches the route pass only with item mesh's routeref change; a compose network alias (jaeger's spm_metrics_source) is declared by no provider", trigger: "a cloud environment running opentelemetry-demo and kube-prometheus for the dynamic reading (no local VM); wave C2's framework routes; ADR-061's .fathom/ zones; a VictoriaMetrics deployment confirmed"}

- [-] Alertmanager beyond the configuration file: template files, AlertmanagerConfig custom resources, and which Alertmanager a Prometheus sends to {id: LB-Q-alertmanager-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "a template glob is an entity joined to nothing: no connector rule discovers a .tmpl file and no dialect reads Go text/template outside Helm's .tpl; an AlertmanagerConfig custom resource (monitoring.coreos.com/v1alpha1) carries a camel-cased route, receiver and matcher schema of its own (matchers as {name, value, matchType} objects) the alertmanager dialect does not read, and an Alertmanager custom resource's own route either; every configuration of a repository routes every alerting rule, since a Prometheus configuration's alerting.alertmanagers is not joined to a configuration file; an alerting rule's runtime labels (its series', the evaluating Prometheus' external labels) are unknown statically, so a matcher on one makes the edge may_route_to, and the measured subjects decide every routing (kube-prometheus: 139 rules, 0 undecided), so the may half is proven on fixtures only", trigger: "an adopter's repository ships Alertmanager templates, AlertmanagerConfig resources or more than one Alertmanager; a subject whose routing reads a runtime label"}
- [-] Grafana beyond the static model walk: a tier-A or -B oracle, Kubernetes volume mounts, legacy string datasources and v2 dashboards in YAML {id: LB-Q-grafana-dashboard-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "no Grafana tool reads a dashboard or a provisioning file offline (Grafana's own source is AGPL-3.0, a behaviour reference only), so the oracle is a walk of its own (tier C, calibration/oracles/grafana) and every level is capped at good; a provider's options.path that a Kubernetes volume mounts (kube-prometheus' /grafana-dashboard-definitions/0) joins nothing, only a compose bind mount maps a container path (counted unmounted); a query whose datasource is a literal name or uid with no type in the dashboard is datasource-unknown (the provisioned datasource's type is declared in another file); a v2 dashboard written in YAML is a Kubernetes object and is not claimed", trigger: "a Grafana CLI or library that loads a dashboard offline under a permissive licence (tier A); the Kubernetes side pass reading a Deployment's volume mounts; a cross-file datasource-type join"}

- [-] Scala at L2 (L1 good, L2 good), below its L4 target, and `.scala` and `.sc` served by the tags dialect: measured 2026-10-10 (wave lf3, fathom bc499d5d) against a scalameta 4.17.4 syntax dump (tier B, definitions and calls, calibration/oracles/scalameta) on typelevel/cats v2.13.0 + http4s/http4s v0.23.38, 1,502 files: sym_recall 1, sym_precision 1, kind_agreement 1, signature_coverage 1, range_agreement .8042, call_recall .9999, call_precision .9999; lang-parity MERGE against scalaextract.go, which is retired (F.54); L3 first read 2026-10-10 (wave lf4 item fileedge, fathom 35bff928, once, K19) against the dump's imports and same-package references (LB-F439): import_resolution .9832, file_edge_precision .9763, file_edge_recall .0112 {id: LB-Q-scala-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L1 is good on range_agreement alone (.8042, 21,084 of 26,218; bar .90): tree-sitter-scala ends an indentation-delimited node (a body on the line after =, a Scala 3 template) at the outdent before the next token, and the tags engine counts a node to its last byte, so such a definition ends on the next definition's line; trimming trailing whitespace in tsEndLine, the engine's file (item tsjs), reads .9511 (O761). L2 is capped at good by its tier-B oracle: no tier-A call oracle runs here. L3 is unmet on file_edge_recall .0112 (329 of 29,261; bar .25) with import_resolution excellent (.9832, LB-F438) and precision .9763: the dialect has no references.scm and stores a call by its callee's last identifier, so a resolved import becomes a file edge only where its name is called bare; 22,563 of the 28,932 undrawn pairs are a wildcard's pairs to every file of a package, 4,292 same-package references, 2,077 named imports (O807). L4 needs scip-java's SemanticDB index, which needs each subject compiled by its own sbt build: sbt is not installed here, and a coursier-launched sbt compiling cross-built cats or http4s for scip-java's SemanticDB is over the wave's one-JVM, 1 GB heap budget (sbt's own launcher defaults to 1 GB before scalac runs), so it was not attempted. universal-ctags 6.2.1 has no Scala parser, so the queue's ctags oracle never existed (O763)", trigger: "the engine's end line trimmed (O761), which reads L1 excellent; a Scala references.scm and qualified call text (`calls.qualify`) under K17/K26's cost gate, then L3 re-read on a holdout; an sbt or Mill build of a Scala subject indexed by scip-java (SemanticDB) for L4"}

- [-] Taskfile beyond the file includes an ingest resolves today: measured against go-task 3.52.0 (task --list-all --json; tier A for definitions and cross-file references; task --dry --force ablation on a copy with every sh: and if: set to true, tier A for references) on go-task/task v3.52.0 (3 Taskfiles fathom reads, of 183), go-task/examples (1) and Runtime-Radar/runtime-radar v0.3.0 (14), 2026-10-02: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (264 definitions), ref_recall 1 and ref_precision .989 (90 of 91 pairs attested; the other is a call to a task the file never declares), xref_precision and xref_recall 1 (14 of 14 include edges), so L1, L2 and L3 excellent; a scratch ingest of the three subjects stores 13 of the 14 edges {id: LB-Q-taskfile-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "a directory an include names resolves to the one doc file in it (design section 4), and the harness resolves over the files the dialect owns while an ingest resolves over every file a doc-engine extractor reads: go-task/task's `taskfile: ./website` names a directory that holds a Taskfile beside a package.json, so the bench scores that edge and an ingest drops it (ingest-side xref_recall .9286, over the L3 excellent line of .80; TestTaskfileDirectoryNeedsTheOneFileRule pins the rule); an include of the repository root as a directory names `.`, which repo-path does not resolve, so only the file form does; an included Taskfile under another name (Taskfile.docker.yml, tasks/build.yml) is not one of go-task's eight basenames and is neither read nor an include target; a namespaced call, an alias and a label are names that match no local task; runtime-radar's kube-manager test-docker calls test-docker-cleanup, which no file declares: the dialect draws it and the tool cannot run it (the one ref_precision miss); the queue's two subjects are thin (the connector never reads testdata, where go-task/task keeps 180 of its 183 Taskfiles) so Runtime-Radar/runtime-radar is added to them and 13 of the 14 scored edges are its", trigger: "fusion.ResolveDocXrefs preferring the file the referring dialect owns when a directory holds several doc files; then the reading is re-measured with a subject that includes directories, and the ingest count read against the reading's"}

the 43 OPA rejects, v0 syntax and examples written to fail, are read and not measured), 2026-10-01: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (2,290 definitions), kind and range agreement 1, signature coverage 1, call_recall and call_precision 1 (2,481 pairs), import_resolution 1 (570 of 570), file_edge_precision .9949, file_edge_recall .3858, so L1 and L2 excellent and L3 good; no file needed a repair (0 of 370 OPA-accepted files) {id: LB-Q-rego-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "file_edge_recall .3858 (581 of 1,506 import-dump pairs) is under the excellent .50: an import of a package split across files names every file of it (regal's data.regal.ast is six files and 160 files import it, data.regal.config two), while a file-resolved edge reaches only the file defining a rule the importer calls or references, so the pairs of files imported and never used stay out; the truth is what the import statements name (LB-F57), not what references reach; 3 of opa's 4 pairs are a key collision (two format testfiles of one directory declaring one package and rule name share a SymbolKey, so a same-file call is scored to the other file); a bare name that refers to a rule of the same package and a reference through an import outside a call are not L2 calls (OPA's compiler, not its parser, tells them from locals); L4 has no producer (no Rego SCIP indexer)", trigger: "the file resolver draws an import statement's own edges, from the importing file to each file of what it names, as LB-F57's truth counts them (an Opus row in fusion's file resolver, for every language at once); or a Rego SCIP producer exists; then L3 is re-measured with the committed oracle"}

the 89 task dependsOn and finalizedBy pairs alone read recall .9888, precision .9670), so L1 and L2 excellent; L3 is measured and unmet {id: LB-Q-gradle-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "xref_recall .0033 (9 of 2,703 oracle cross-file references, include and project dependency to the named project's build script) at xref_precision 1: imports.scm resolves every path to its script by Gradle's default layout (namespace-dir over ':'), but the file resolver binds a call through a whole-module import only to the target file's one top-level symbol, and a build script declares many (plugins, dependencies, tasks) and none named by its project path (TestIngest_GradleBuildReachesTheGradleDialect pins the rule); a projectDir or buildFileName override and a relative project('x') are not resolved either; the Kotlin DSL (build.gradle.kts, settings.gradle.kts: spring-boot's 53 scripts, every okhttp script) stays with the kotlin dialect, because a second dialect on the kotlin blob needs its own queue entry and the file resolver keys a file by extension, so a .kts script would resolve under kotlin's strategy (fusion's resolver and the engine, not this item's files); the grammar reads 16 of the 444 measured scripts with an error (3.6%), but 82 of grails-core v7.2.4's 343 build scripts (23.9%) and 6 of spock-2.4's 25, mostly a comma between a project dependency and its closure; LB-Q-gradle also needs LB-Q-kotlin, open at its L4 target (G2-6)", trigger: "a file-resolved binding from an import to its target file as a whole (a root entity per file the resolver can bind a call to, an Opus row in fusion's file resolver), then L3 re-measured with the committed oracle; a gradle-kts dialect on the kotlin blob (queue entry, basenames build.gradle.kts and settings.gradle.kts, a file-resolve key that is not the extension) measured with the same oracle on JetBrains/Exposed and square/okhttp; LB-Q-kotlin at L4 for this row"}

The cross-file truth is tier C, which caps at good (cfn-lint resolves no nested template, no identity join and no handler; an independent tier-A or tier-B resolver of a SAM application's handlers, sam build's own, needs each runtime's toolchain). And 25 of the 404 handler bindings are missed, every one a node handler the javascript dialect does not extract (an export whose value is a wrapper call, module.exports.x = async () => {}: O721, the javascript dialect's to fix), which also caps xref_recall under the .95 a tier-A truth would ask of excellent. Not read: a Fn::Sub's ${} names outside the CloudWatch properties, Fn::FindInMap, Fn::ImportValue and Export (cross-stack), the go, provided, dotnet and custom runtimes' handlers (a binary or an assembly), a template whose YAML cfn-lint rejects (O722)", trigger: "a tier-A or tier-B handler or nested-template resolver an oracle can run offline, or O721 fixed in the javascript dialect"}

126 units Terragrunt could not evaluate and 13 files the parser rejects are left out), sym_recall and sym_precision 1, range_agreement 1 (3460 entities), ref_recall 1 and ref_precision 1 (362 pairs), xref_precision 0.9876 and xref_recall 0.9917 (479 of 483 truth pairs found, 479 of 485 found in truth) against python-hcl2 8.1.4 (tier C: definitions and references) and terragrunt 1.1.6 render --json --with-metadata and find --json --include (tier A: include, dependency, terraform.source and input edges) {id: LB-Q-terragrunt-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L1 and L2 capped at good: no tool reports a Terragrunt file's entities or the references between them with lines, so the independent parse is tier C (§1.3); wave ox measured a tier-A attestation (calibration/oracles/terragrunt/terragrunt_attest.py: render --with-metadata found_in_file for blocks, inputs and a unit's own locals, find --include for includes, ablation for an included file's locals, feature blocks, unnamed files and every reference; names and lines from python-hcl2, since fathom's dialect and Terragrunt share hashicorp/hcl/v2): it evidences all 74 definitions and 20 references of the live example but only 3,056 of 3,571 definitions and 246 of 342 references of test/fixtures (152 terragrunt.stack.hcl files render does not read, 78 other files no rendered unit names, 10 units that do not render, 174 included-file locals and 96 references no rendered output depends on), so the families stay at C, locals with them; L3 misses 4 truth pairs: a config_path read from another include (include.x.dependency.y.config_path, include.x.inputs.p) or from get_parent_terragrunt_dir(), and 6 extra edges where find_in_parent_folders finds the file at two ancestors and Terragrunt takes the nearer (one file cannot see which ancestors have it); an included file's relative paths are left unresolved (they are read from the including unit)", trigger: "a tier-A dump of a Terragrunt file's blocks with lines, or a stack render (terragrunt stack generate offline) and a fixtures scope the owner accepts for the attestation oracle; fusion.ResolveDocXrefs choosing the nearest of several repo-path candidates (tl); read_terragrunt_config edges, which terragrunt find --reading reports per unit, not per entity"}

re-examined in wave ox with the owner-approved packer 1.16.1 and nomad 2.0.7 release binaries (local measuring tools only, sha256 in calibration/oracles/hcl-tools/README.md): packer inspect without plugins lists only variables and the locals it can evaluate (chef/bento: 139 input variables, 4 locals, an empty builds section;
